People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. situations when remaining seated is expected.\n  manifestation: During longer classes or vendor meetings, I keep finding reasons to get up, like checking my inventory box, refilling water, or walking What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’m having a hard time keeping things organized. Like my schoolwork and this small accessories shop I’m trying to manage… the supplies, due dates, receipts, notes for assignments—everything just ends up in piles, and then I don’t really know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or stressed because of these organizational difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does get pretty overwhelming. It’s like my school stuff and the shop stuff all mix together—supplies, receipts, due dates, notes—and then it’s just piles, and I kind of freeze because I don’t know what to start with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty often. Like in class, or if I’m watching a video for business stuff, I’ll be listening at first, but then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or order ideas, and I realize I missed a lot of what was said.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Even if I’m just sitting there, it kind of feels like my body is buzzing. My mom still has some notes from elementary school… like teachers saying I was always getting out of my seat, talking before they called on me, and rushing through group things even when I knew the work.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks in multiple areas of your life, such as school and work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I don’t remember all of it, but I was always kind of restless. I mean… I get annoyed sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than anyone else. Then I realize I missed a lot of what was said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it is causing problems. I don’t blame them, but… it’s not great.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention or following instructions at home, besides the problems you mentioned at school and with your shop?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like my schoolwork and this small accessories shop I’m trying to manage… the supplies, due dates, receipts, notes for assignments—everything just ends up in piles, and then I don’t really know what I’m supposed to do first.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or stressed because of these organizational difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does get pretty overwhelming. It’s like my school stuff and the shop stuff all mix together—supplies, receipts, due dates, notes—and then it’s just piles, and I kind of freeze because I don’t know what to start with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty often. Like in class, or if I’m watching a video for business stuff, I’ll be listening at first, but then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or order ideas, and I realize I missed a lot of what was said.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Even if I’m just sitting there, it kind of feels like my body is buzzing. Like I need to move or shift around, especially when I’m supposed to sit still and listen.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in before someone’s even finished talking. Then I realize I kind of guessed what they were going to say, and it’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had these difficulties with organization and focus, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been there since I was little. My mom still has some notes from elementary school… like teachers saying I was always getting out of my seat, talking before they called on me, and rushing through group things even when I knew the work.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks in multiple areas of your life, such as school and work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. If a teacher, or like someone in a vendor meeting, gives me a few steps, I’ll do the first part and think I’m done. Then later I find out I missed something important… like attaching a form or finishing the last part of an order note.\n\nDoctor: Did you exhibit a lot of physical activity as a child, such as running, climbing, or having trouble playing quietly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t remember all of it, but I was always kind of restless. Even now, if I’m supposed to sit still and listen, it feels like my body is buzzing and I need to move.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble managing your emotions, like getting really angry or upset for no reason?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I get annoyed sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than anyone else. I haven’t really thought about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble staying focused or paying attention in multiple areas of your life, such as both at school and at your accessories shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like in class, or if I’m watching a video for my business assignments, I’ll be listening at first, but then after a few minutes I’m thinking about charm designs or order ideas. Then I realize I missed a lot of what was said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it is causing problems. I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk. And with the shop stuff, people I work with don’t really depend on me as much anymore. I don’t blame them, but… it’s not great.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention or following instructions at home, besides the problems you mentioned at school and with your shop?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. At home it’s less obvious, I guess. I don’t… really notice instructions the same way there. Maybe sometimes, but I can’t think of a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention or following instructions at home?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. At home… I guess I don’t notice it as much. I can’t think of a specific time where that happened.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble paying attention or following instructions in other areas of your life besides school and your accessories shop?\n\nPatient: Not really sure… outside of those, I don’t have a clear example. I mean, maybe it happens sometimes, but I don’t really notice it.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity causing problems in your friendships or romantic relationships?\n\nPatient: Not really romantic stuff… but with people I do shop projects with, yeah, a little. I think they’ve started relying on me less because I miss things or don’t follow through the right way. And it’s caused problems with my program too — like I missed some required steps, and now my financial aid might be at risk.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble paying attention or following instructions in social situations with friends?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. With friends, I don’t think I notice it that much… like, nothing specific comes to mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s like my school stuff and the shop stuff all mix together—supplies, receipts, due dates, notes—and then it’s just piles, and I kind of freeze because I don’t know what to start with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty often. Like in class, or if I’m watching a video for business stuff, I’ll be listening at first, but then a few minutes later I’m thinking about charm designs or order ideas, and I realize I missed a lot of what was said.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Even if I’m just sitting there, it kind of feels like my body is buzzing. Like in class discussions or critiques, I’ll jump in before someone’s even finished talking.
